Not really… I mean, I used to have little stuff. Like gallery nights, or playing basketball after work, or going to get those food-truck tacos on Thursdays. There’s this one truck that used to put, like, pickled onions on everything, and I’d always tell people it was worth the line.

I mean… I guess I’d call it mild compared to, like, not getting out of bed at all or something. I’m still going to work. I clock in, I do the floor stuff, talk to customers when I have to.

But it is messing with things. I’ve been screwing up inventory counts and display plans, like little numbers or where things are supposed to go, and my manager has had to double-check me more. Which is embarrassing, because displays used to be the part I was actually decent at.

And outside work I’ve just been… not showing up. Gallery nights, basketball, the youth art workshops—I used to go to those without thinking about it. Now I skip them, or I tell myself I’ll go next week. So yeah, it’s not huge maybe, but it’s definitely getting in the way.

inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep thinking I do not deserve the award or the chance at supervisor, and I feel guilty like I tricked everyone even though people tell me I did not.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: At work I keep losing my place on simple stuff, like counting inventory or picking colors for a display, and I end up second-guessing myself over things I used to do fast.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: Before I started feeling this low after the award attention, I never believed coworkers were building a case against me or that my art was stolen. Those thoughts only showed up once I stopped enjoying things and started sleeping and eating badly.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’m still going to work, but I’ve been messing up inventory counts and display plans enough that my manager has had to double-check me. I’ve also been skipping gallery nights, basketball, and the youth art workshops I used to show up for.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two months, since the window display award and all the attention from the district manager.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 25 / retail_salesperson / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, death is one of those things you hear about all the time, like somebody’s aunt or someone from school, and then you end up thinking about how people just disappear, you know?\n\nBut if you mean, like… am I planning something, I don’t— I’m not sure. I haven’t really sat down and made some kind of plan or anything. Sometimes my mind just kind of goes off in weird directions, and then I get distracted by other stuff, like work schedules or whether I left my laundry in the washer too long. So… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking about suicide as a way to escape your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “escape” sounds so definite, like I sat there and decided that’s what it would be for. I don’t think I’ve thought it through like that.\n\nSometimes I just want everything to stop for a while, like when the store lights are too bright and the music keeps looping the same five songs, and then someone asks if we have something in the back even though the tag says we don’t—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I’ve had thoughts that are kind of… dark, but I don’t know if I’d call it thinking about suicide as an escape. It’s more like my brain drifts there and then away again.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of sadness or being flat are mild, or are they interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d call it mild compared to, like, not getting out of bed at all or something. I’m still going to work. I clock in, I do the floor stuff, talk to customers when I have to.\n\nBut it is messing with things. I’ve been screwing up inventory counts and display plans, like little numbers or where things are supposed to go, and my manager has had to double-check me more. Which is embarrassing, because displays used to be the part I was actually decent at.\n\nAnd outside work I’ve just been… not showing up. Gallery nights, basketball, the youth art workshops—I used to go to those without thinking about it. Now I skip them, or I tell myself I’ll go next week. So yeah, it’s not huge maybe, but it’s definitely getting in the way.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you say these feelings of being flat and not enjoying things are affecting your daily life more than your ability to experience pleasure?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think it’s more the not enjoying things part, if that makes sense. Like, I can still get through the day, mostly, but the stuff that used to actually feel good just kind of doesn’t land anymore.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly I’m here because I’m not eating right. Like at work, on my lunch break, I’ll buy something I used to actually like, and then I just sit there staring at it. Half the time I throw most of it away. It’s weird because lunch used to be the one decent part of the shift, you know, especially when the store’s busy and people are asking where stuff is like I know every aisle by heart… but yeah, I just barely want to eat anymore, and that kind of scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m like crying all the time or anything. It’s more like I’m just kind of going through the day. Work is weird because the lights are so bright in there, and they play the same songs over and over—there’s this one song that comes on and I swear it makes the whole shift feel longer.\n\nSo… sad, maybe? I’m not really sure how to answer it. I just know I haven’t felt like myself lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer enjoying activities that you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually look forward to stuff, like gallery nights or playing basketball, and there’s this food truck with tacos I used to go out of my way for. I even liked helping kids with art stuff sometimes, like little projects, because they get so excited over the smallest things.\n\nBut lately I’ll go and just kind of… stand there. Like I’m there, but I’m not really into it. Then I start thinking about how long it’ll take to get home or whether people can tell I’m acting weird, and I’ll make some excuse to leave early.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep trying to do the same stuff. Like gallery nights used to be kind of cool, and basketball, and there’s this taco truck I’d always talk myself into stopping at even if it was out of the way.\n\nBut now I’ll go and it’s like… I’m just standing there. Not mad, not happy, just flat. Even helping kids with art stuff, which used to be fun because they get so into it, I just feel like I’m waiting for it to be over. Then I usually make up some reason I have to leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about, like, a number on a scale. I haven’t really been checking. But I’m barely eating lately, honestly. Like at work, on my lunch break, I’ll get something I used to like and just sit there staring at it, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nThat’s kind of what made me think maybe I should talk to somebody, because it feels stupid to buy food and then not even want it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… about two months? It kind of started after that window display award thing at work. The district manager made a big deal about it, and everyone kept looking at me and talking about it, which should’ve felt good, I guess.\n\nI don’t know, after that it’s like everything started feeling off, and it just hasn’t really gone back. So… yeah, around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I keep thinking I didn’t really earn that award at work. Like everyone made it into this big thing, and now there’s talk about maybe giving me a shot at supervisor, and I just keep feeling like… why me?\n\nPeople tell me I did a good job and that I’m not faking anything, but it feels like I fooled them somehow. Like I tricked everybody into thinking I’m better than I am. And then I feel guilty about that, like I’m taking something that should go to someone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for most of the day, every day, over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s been pretty much since that whole window display award thing, when the district manager made such a big deal and everybody was paying attention to me.\n\nIt’s hard to say “every day” exactly, because some parts of the day I’m just working and doing what I have to do, and customers come in asking where stuff is, or complaining about prices, and I kind of just go on autopilot. But it’s been there most days, most of the day, for about two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have little stuff. I’ll go if someone asks, but I feel flat, like I’m watching it instead of being in it. Then I usually make some excuse and leave early.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. I think… yeah, maybe a little? It’s hard to tell because it’s not like one day it suddenly changed.

It’s more like I used to actually look forward to stuff—gallery nights, basketball, getting tacos from the food trucks after, helping kids with art projects—and lately I’ll show up and just kind of stand there feeling flat. Like I’m there but not really in it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly I’m here because I’m not eating right. Like at work, on my lunch break, I’ll buy something I used to actually like, and then I just sit there staring at it. Half the time I throw most of it away. It’s weird because lunch used to be the one decent part of the shift, you know, especially when the store’s busy and people are asking where stuff is like I know every aisle by heart… but yeah, I just barely want to eat anymore, and that kind of scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m like crying all the time or anything. It’s more like I’m just kind of going through the day. Work is weird because the lights are so bright in there, and they play the same songs over and over—there’s this one song that comes on and I swear it makes the whole shift feel longer.\n\nSo… sad, maybe? I’m not really sure how to answer it. I just know I haven’t felt like myself lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer enjoying activities that you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually look forward to stuff, like gallery nights or playing basketball, and there’s this food truck with tacos I used to go out of my way for. I even liked helping kids with art stuff sometimes, like little projects, because they get so excited over the smallest things.\n\nBut lately I’ll go and just kind of… stand there. Like I’m there, but I’m not really into it. Then I start thinking about how long it’ll take to get home or whether people can tell I’m acting weird, and I’ll make some excuse to leave early.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep trying to do the same stuff. Like gallery nights used to be kind of cool, and basketball, and there’s this taco truck I’d always talk myself into stopping at even if it was out of the way.\n\nBut now I’ll go and it’s like… I’m just standing there. Not mad, not happy, just flat. Even helping kids with art stuff, which used to be fun because they get so into it, I just feel like I’m waiting for it to be over. Then I usually make up some reason I have to leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about, like, a number on a scale. I haven’t really been checking. But I’m barely eating lately, honestly. Like at work, on my lunch break, I’ll get something I used to like and just sit there staring at it, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nThat’s kind of what made me think maybe I should talk to somebody, because it feels stupid to buy food and then not even want it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… about two months? It kind of started after that window display award thing at work. The district manager made a big deal about it, and everyone kept looking at me and talking about it, which should’ve felt good, I guess.\n\nI don’t know, after that it’s like everything started feeling off, and it just hasn’t really gone back. So… yeah, around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I keep thinking I didn’t really earn that award at work. Like everyone made it into this big thing, and now there’s talk about maybe giving me a shot at supervisor, and I just keep feeling like… why me?\n\nPeople tell me I did a good job and that I’m not faking anything, but it feels like I fooled them somehow. Like I tricked everybody into thinking I’m better than I am. And then I feel guilty about that, like I’m taking something that should go to someone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for most of the day, every day, over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s been pretty much since that whole window display award thing, when the district manager made such a big deal and everybody was paying attention to me.\n\nIt’s hard to say “every day” exactly, because some parts of the day I’m just working and doing what I have to do, and customers come in asking where stuff is, or complaining about prices, and I kind of just go on autopilot. But it’s been there most days, most of the day, for about two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have little stuff. I’ll go if someone asks, but I feel flat, like I’m watching it instead of being in it. Then I usually make some excuse and leave early.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, death is one of those things you hear about all the time, like somebody’s aunt or someone from school, and then you end up thinking about how people just disappear, you know?\n\nBut if you mean, like… am I planning something, I don’t— I’m not sure. I haven’t really sat down and made some kind of plan or anything. Sometimes my mind just kind of goes off in weird directions, and then I get distracted by other stuff, like work schedules or whether I left my laundry in the washer too long. So… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking about suicide as a way to escape your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “escape” sounds so definite, like I sat there and decided that’s what it would be for. I don’t think I’ve thought it through like that.\n\nSometimes I just want everything to stop for a while, like when the store lights are too bright and the music keeps looping the same five songs, and then someone asks if we have something in the back even though the tag says we don’t—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I’ve had thoughts that are kind of… dark, but I don’t know if I’d call it thinking about suicide as an escape. It’s more like my brain drifts there and then away again.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of sadness or being flat are mild, or are they interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d call it mild compared to, like, not getting out of bed at all or something. Not really… I mean, I still do some of the same stuff, but I don’t know if I’d say I enjoy it.

Like gallery nights, basketball, grabbing tacos from the food trucks after—there’s one place that used to be my favorite, I’d always tell people the salsa was worth it, even if they complained about it being too hot. And helping kids with art projects, too, that used to make me feel good, like I was doing something that mattered.

Now I’ll be there and I’m just kind of… standing around. Flat. Watching everybody else have a normal time. Then I usually start thinking of some reason to leave early, like I forgot something or I’m tired. So, no, not much really feels enjoyable lately.

I haven’t really sat down and made some kind of plan or anything. Sometimes my mind just kind of goes off in weird directions, and then I get distracted by other stuff, like work schedules or whether I left my laundry in the washer too long. So… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking about suicide as a way to escape your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “escape” sounds so definite, like I sat there and decided that’s what it would be for. I don’t think I’ve thought it through like that.\n\nSometimes I just want everything to stop for a while, like when the store lights are too bright and the music keeps looping the same five songs, and then someone asks if we have something in the back even though the tag says we don’t—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I’ve had thoughts that are kind of… dark, but I don’t know if I’d call it thinking about suicide as an escape. It’s more like my brain drifts there and then away again.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of sadness or being flat are mild, or are they interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d call it mild compared to, like, not getting out of bed at all or something. I’m still going to work. I clock in, I do the floor stuff, talk to customers when I have to.\n\nBut it is messing with things. I’ve been screwing up inventory counts and display plans, like little numbers or where things are supposed to go, and my manager has had to double-check me more. Which is embarrassing, because displays used to be the part I was actually decent at.\n\nAnd outside work I’ve just been… not showing up. Gallery nights, basketball, the youth art workshops—I used to go to those without thinking about it. Now I skip them, or I tell myself I’ll go next week. So yeah, it’s not huge maybe, but it’s definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of being flat and not enjoying things are affecting your daily life more than your ability to experience pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s more the not enjoying things part, if that makes sense. Like, I can still get through the day, mostly, but the stuff that used to actually feel good just kind of doesn’t land anymore.\n\nI used to get excited about gallery nights, or playing basketball, or even just getting tacos from the food trucks after. And helping kids with art stuff, that used to make me feel useful, like I was… I don’t know, part of something. Lately I’ll go and just stand there feeling flat, like I’m watching it happen from somewhere else, and then I usually make some excuse to leave early. Like I forgot something, or I’m tired, or whatever.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness or being flat are getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, maybe a little? It’s hard to tell because it’s not like one day it suddenly changed.\n\nIt’s more like I used to actually look forward to stuff—gallery nights, basketball, getting tacos from the food trucks after, helping kids with art projects—and lately I’ll show up and just kind of stand there feeling flat. Like I’m there but not really in it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about some excuse to leave, like I’ve got laundry or I forgot something at home. Which is stupid, because I used to be the one trying to get other people to go. So… yeah, I guess it feels like it’s been getting worse, or at least happening more.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness or being flat are severe, or are they more on the mild to moderate side?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, probably. Maybe mild to moderate on bad days, but not like… severe severe.\n\nIt’s more that things don’t feel right anymore. Like I used to get excited for gallery nights, basketball, grabbing tacos from the food trucks after—there’s this one truck with the green salsa that, I don’t know, everyone acts like it’s too spicy but it’s not that bad—and helping kids with art stuff used to make me feel good. Lately I’m there and I just kind of stand around feeling flat, like I’m waiting for it to be over, and then I usually make up some excuse to leave. So yeah… not severe, but it’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still do some of the same stuff, but I don’t know if I’d say I enjoy it.\n\nLike gallery nights, basketball, grabbing tacos from the food trucks after—there’s one place that used to be my favorite, I’d always tell people the salsa was worth it, even if they complained about it being too hot. And helping kids with art projects, too, that used to make me feel good, like I was doing something that mattered.\n\nNow I’ll be there and I’m just kind of… standing around. Flat. Watching everybody else have a normal time. Then I usually start thinking of some reason to leave early, like I forgot something or I’m tired. So, no, not much really feels enjoyable lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Probably… about two months? Around then.

It was after that whole window display award thing at work, when the district manager started paying attention and everyone kept bringing it up. Which sounds like it should’ve been a good thing, right? Like people were congratulating me, and I remember one coworker kept joking like I was some kind of big artist now, which was weird because it was just a display, not like a gallery piece or anything.

But yeah, since around then, maybe two months, things I used to like just started feeling kind of dull.

Watching everybody else have a normal time. Then I usually start thinking of some reason to leave early, like I forgot something or I’m tired. So, no, not much really feels enjoyable lately.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling like you're not enjoying the things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Probably… about two months? Around then.\n\nIt was after that whole window display award thing at work, when the district manager started paying attention and everyone kept bringing it up. Which sounds like it should’ve been a good thing, right? Like people were congratulating me, and I remember one coworker kept joking like I was some kind of big artist now, which was weird because it was just a display, not like a gallery piece or anything.\n\nBut yeah, since around then, maybe two months, things I used to like just started feeling kind of dull.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really, no. I still show up to some things, like gallery nights or basketball, but it’s not the same. I used to actually look forward to that stuff.

Like after basketball we’d grab tacos from the food trucks, and there was this one place everyone liked—well, I liked it more than everybody else probably because of the salsa—and I’d be excited about it before. Or helping kids with art projects, that used to feel good.

Now I’m just kind of there… standing around feeling flat, watching everyone else have fun. Then I usually make up some reason to leave early. So I guess I still do things sometimes, but I don’t really feel much pleasure from them.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… mostly I’m here because I’m not eating right. Like at work, on my lunch break, I’ll buy something I used to actually like, and then I just sit there staring at it. Half the time I throw most of it away. It’s weird because lunch used to be the one decent part of the shift, you know, especially when the store’s busy and people are asking where stuff is like I know every aisle by heart… but yeah, I just barely want to eat anymore, and that kind of scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m like crying all the time or anything. It’s more like I’m just kind of going through the day. Work is weird because the lights are so bright in there, and they play the same songs over and over—there’s this one song that comes on and I swear it makes the whole shift feel longer.\n\nSo… sad, maybe? I’m not really sure how to answer it. I just know I haven’t felt like myself lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer enjoying activities that you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually look forward to stuff, like gallery nights or playing basketball, and there’s this food truck with tacos I used to go out of my way for. I even liked helping kids with art stuff sometimes, like little projects, because they get so excited over the smallest things.\n\nBut lately I’ll go and just kind of… stand there. Like I’m there, but I’m not really into it. Then I start thinking about how long it’ll take to get home or whether people can tell I’m acting weird, and I’ll make some excuse to leave early.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep trying to do the same stuff. Like gallery nights used to be kind of cool, and basketball, and there’s this taco truck I’d always talk myself into stopping at even if it was out of the way.\n\nBut now I’ll go and it’s like… I’m just standing there. Not mad, not happy, just flat. Even helping kids with art stuff, which used to be fun because they get so into it, I just feel like I’m waiting for it to be over. Then I usually make up some reason I have to leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about, like, a number on a scale. I haven’t really been checking. But I’m barely eating lately, honestly. Like at work, on my lunch break, I’ll get something I used to like and just sit there staring at it, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nThat’s kind of what made me think maybe I should talk to somebody, because it feels stupid to buy food and then not even want it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… about two months? It kind of started after that window display award thing at work. The district manager made a big deal about it, and everyone kept looking at me and talking about it, which should’ve felt good, I guess.\n\nI don’t know, after that it’s like everything started feeling off, and it just hasn’t really gone back. So… yeah, around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I keep thinking I didn’t really earn that award at work. Like everyone made it into this big thing, and now there’s talk about maybe giving me a shot at supervisor, and I just keep feeling like… why me?\n\nPeople tell me I did a good job and that I’m not faking anything, but it feels like I fooled them somehow. Like I tricked everybody into thinking I’m better than I am. And then I feel guilty about that, like I’m taking something that should go to someone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for most of the day, every day, over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s been pretty much since that whole window display award thing, when the district manager made such a big deal and everybody was paying attention to me.\n\nIt’s hard to say “every day” exactly, because some parts of the day I’m just working and doing what I have to do, and customers come in asking where stuff is, or complaining about prices, and I kind of just go on autopilot. But it’s been there most days, most of the day, for about two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have little stuff. Like gallery nights, or playing basketball after work, or going to get those food-truck tacos on Thursdays. There’s this one truck that used to put, like, pickled onions on everything, and I’d always tell people it was worth the line.\n\nAnd I used to help out with kids’ art stuff sometimes, like showing them how to make displays or draw signs, and that usually made me feel… I don’t know, useful maybe.\n\nBut lately I just kind of stand there. I’ll go if someone asks, but I feel flat, like I’m watching it instead of being in it. Then I usually make some excuse and leave early.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, death is one of those things you hear about all the time, like somebody’s aunt or someone from school, and then you end up thinking about how people just disappear, you know?\n\nBut if you mean, like… am I planning something, I don’t— I’m not sure. I haven’t really sat down and made some kind of plan or anything. Sometimes my mind just kind of goes off in weird directions, and then I get distracted by other stuff, like work schedules or whether I left my laundry in the washer too long. So… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking about suicide as a way to escape your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “escape” sounds so definite, like I sat there and decided that’s what it would be for. I don’t think I’ve thought it through like that.\n\nSometimes I just want everything to stop for a while, like when the store lights are too bright and the music keeps looping the same five songs, and then someone asks if we have something in the back even though the tag says we don’t—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I’ve had thoughts that are kind of… dark, but I don’t know if I’d call it thinking about suicide as an escape. It’s more like my brain drifts there and then away again.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of sadness or being flat are mild, or are they interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d call it mild compared to, like, not getting out of bed at all or something. I’m still going to work. I clock in, I do the floor stuff, talk to customers when I have to.\n\nBut it is messing with things. I’ve been screwing up inventory counts and display plans, like little numbers or where things are supposed to go, and my manager has had to double-check me more. Which is embarrassing, because displays used to be the part I was actually decent at.\n\nAnd outside work I’ve just been… not showing up. Gallery nights, basketball, the youth art workshops—I used to go to those without thinking about it. Now I skip them, or I tell myself I’ll go next week. So yeah, it’s not huge maybe, but it’s definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of being flat and not enjoying things are affecting your daily life more than your ability to experience pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s more the not enjoying things part, if that makes sense. Like, I can still get through the day, mostly, but the stuff that used to actually feel good just kind of doesn’t land anymore.\n\nI used to get excited about gallery nights, or playing basketball, or even just getting tacos from the food trucks after. And helping kids with art stuff, that used to make me feel useful, like I was… I don’t know, part of something. Lately I’ll go and just stand there feeling flat, like I’m watching it happen from somewhere else, and then I usually make some excuse to leave early. Like I forgot something, or I’m tired, or whatever.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness or being flat are getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, maybe a little? It’s hard to tell because it’s not like one day it suddenly changed.\n\nIt’s more like I used to actually look forward to stuff—gallery nights, basketball, getting tacos from the food trucks after, helping kids with art projects—and lately I’ll show up and just kind of stand there feeling flat. Like I’m there but not really in it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about some excuse to leave, like I’ve got laundry or I forgot something at home. Which is stupid, because I used to be the one trying to get other people to go. So… yeah, I guess it feels like it’s been getting worse, or at least happening more.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness or being flat are severe, or are they more on the mild to moderate side?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, probably. Maybe mild to moderate on bad days, but not like… severe severe.\n\nIt’s more that things don’t feel right anymore. Like I used to get excited for gallery nights, basketball, grabbing tacos from the food trucks after—there’s this one truck with the green salsa that, I don’t know, everyone acts like it’s too spicy but it’s not that bad—and helping kids with art stuff used to make me feel good. Lately I’m there and I just kind of stand around feeling flat, like I’m waiting for it to be over, and then I usually make up some excuse to leave. Yeah… I mean, I’m still functioning, I guess. I still go to work and all that, so it’s not like I’m just completely gone.

But I’ve been messing things up more. Inventory counts, display plans… stuff I normally could do without thinking too hard. My manager’s been double-checking me, which is embarrassing. He tries to act casual about it, like, “Let’s just look this over,” but I know what that means.

And outside work, I’ve been skipping a lot. Gallery nights, basketball, the youth art workshops… I used to be pretty consistent with those. I even had this one kid at the workshop who always wanted to use way too much glitter, like the whole table would be covered in it, and I’d complain but it was kind of funny. Now I just don’t show up half the time.

So… yeah, I guess it is interfering. Not totally destroying everything, but enough that people notice.

I just know I haven’t felt like myself lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer enjoying activities that you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I used to actually look forward to stuff, like gallery nights or playing basketball, and there’s this food truck with tacos I used to go out of my way for. I even liked helping kids with art stuff sometimes, like little projects, because they get so excited over the smallest things.\n\nBut lately I’ll go and just kind of… stand there. Like I’m there, but I’m not really into it. Then I start thinking about how long it’ll take to get home or whether people can tell I’m acting weird, and I’ll make some excuse to leave early.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I keep trying to do the same stuff. Like gallery nights used to be kind of cool, and basketball, and there’s this taco truck I’d always talk myself into stopping at even if it was out of the way.\n\nBut now I’ll go and it’s like… I’m just standing there. Not mad, not happy, just flat. Even helping kids with art stuff, which used to be fun because they get so into it, I just feel like I’m waiting for it to be over. Then I usually make up some reason I have to leave.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about, like, a number on a scale. I haven’t really been checking. But I’m barely eating lately, honestly. Like at work, on my lunch break, I’ll get something I used to like and just sit there staring at it, and then I end up throwing most of it away.\n\nThat’s kind of what made me think maybe I should talk to somebody, because it feels stupid to buy food and then not even want it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Maybe… about two months? It kind of started after that window display award thing at work. The district manager made a big deal about it, and everyone kept looking at me and talking about it, which should’ve felt good, I guess.\n\nI don’t know, after that it’s like everything started feeling off, and it just hasn’t really gone back. So… yeah, around two months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling worthless or guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… kind of. I keep thinking I didn’t really earn that award at work. Like everyone made it into this big thing, and now there’s talk about maybe giving me a shot at supervisor, and I just keep feeling like… why me?\n\nPeople tell me I did a good job and that I’m not faking anything, but it feels like I fooled them somehow. Like I tricked everybody into thinking I’m better than I am. And then I feel guilty about that, like I’m taking something that should go to someone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for most of the day, every day, over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s been pretty much since that whole window display award thing, when the district manager made such a big deal and everybody was paying attention to me.\n\nIt’s hard to say “every day” exactly, because some parts of the day I’m just working and doing what I have to do, and customers come in asking where stuff is, or complaining about prices, and I kind of just go on autopilot. But it’s been there most days, most of the day, for about two months.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still give you pleasure?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I used to have little stuff. Like gallery nights, or playing basketball after work, or going to get those food-truck tacos on Thursdays. There’s this one truck that used to put, like, pickled onions on everything, and I’d always tell people it was worth the line.\n\nAnd I used to help out with kids’ art stuff sometimes, like showing them how to make displays or draw signs, and that usually made me feel… I don’t know, useful maybe.\n\nBut lately I just kind of stand there. I’ll go if someone asks, but I feel flat, like I’m watching it instead of being in it. Then I usually make some excuse and leave early.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that. I mean, death is one of those things you hear about all the time, like somebody’s aunt or someone from school, and then you end up thinking about how people just disappear, you know?\n\nBut if you mean, like… am I planning something, I don’t— I’m not sure. I haven’t really sat down and made some kind of plan or anything. Sometimes my mind just kind of goes off in weird directions, and then I get distracted by other stuff, like work schedules or whether I left my laundry in the washer too long. So… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Have you been thinking about suicide as a way to escape your current feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “escape” sounds so definite, like I sat there and decided that’s what it would be for. I don’t think I’ve thought it through like that.\n\nSometimes I just want everything to stop for a while, like when the store lights are too bright and the music keeps looping the same five songs, and then someone asks if we have something in the back even though the tag says we don’t—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I’ve had thoughts that are kind of… dark, but I don’t know if I’d call it thinking about suicide as an escape. It’s more like my brain drifts there and then away again.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your feelings of sadness or being flat are mild, or are they interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I’d call it mild compared to, like, not getting out of bed at all or something. I’m still going to work. I clock in, I do the floor stuff, talk to customers when I have to.\n\nBut it is messing with things. I’ve been screwing up inventory counts and display plans, like little numbers or where things are supposed to go, and my manager has had to double-check me more. Which is embarrassing, because displays used to be the part I was actually decent at.\n\nAnd outside work I’ve just been… not showing up. Gallery nights, basketball, the youth art workshops—I used to go to those without thinking about it. Now I skip them, or I tell myself I’ll go next week. So yeah, it’s not huge maybe, but it’s definitely getting in the way.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of being flat and not enjoying things are affecting your daily life more than your ability to experience pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s more the not enjoying things part, if that makes sense. Like, I can still get through the day, mostly, but the stuff that used to actually feel good just kind of doesn’t land anymore.\n\nI used to get excited about gallery nights, or playing basketball, or even just getting tacos from the food trucks after. And helping kids with art stuff, that used to make me feel useful, like I was… I don’t know, part of something. Lately I’ll go and just stand there feeling flat, like I’m watching it happen from somewhere else, and then I usually make some excuse to leave early. Like I forgot something, or I’m tired, or whatever.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness or being flat are getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, maybe a little? It’s hard to tell because it’s not like one day it suddenly changed.\n\nIt’s more like I used to actually look forward to stuff—gallery nights, basketball, getting tacos from the food trucks after, helping kids with art projects—and lately I’ll show up and just kind of stand there feeling flat. Like I’m there but not really in it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about some excuse to leave, like I’ve got laundry or I forgot something at home. Which is stupid, because I used to be the one trying to get other people to go. So… yeah, I guess it feels like it’s been getting worse, or at least happening more.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings of sadness or being flat are severe, or are they more on the mild to moderate side?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, probably. Maybe mild to moderate on bad days, but not like… severe severe.\n\nIt’s more that things don’t feel right anymore. Like I used to get excited for gallery nights, basketball, grabbing tacos from the food trucks after—there’s this one truck with the green salsa that, I don’t know, everyone acts like it’s too spicy but it’s not that bad—and helping kids with art stuff used to make me feel good. Lately I’m there and I just kind of stand around feeling flat, like I’m waiting for it to be over, and then I usually make up some excuse to leave. So yeah… not severe, but it’s there.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I still do some of the same stuff, but I don’t know if I’d say I enjoy it.\n\nLike gallery nights, basketball, grabbing tacos from the food trucks after—there’s one place that used to be my favorite, I’d always tell people the salsa was worth it, even if they complained about it being too hot. And helping kids with art projects, too, that used to make me feel good, like I was doing something that mattered.\n\nNow I’ll be there and I’m just kind of… standing around. Flat. Watching everybody else have a normal time. Then I usually start thinking of some reason to leave early, like I forgot something or I’m tired. So, no, not much really feels enjoyable lately.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you been feeling like you're not enjoying the things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Probably… about two months? Around then.\n\nIt was after that whole window display award thing at work, when the district manager started paying attention and everyone kept bringing it up. Which sounds like it should’ve been a good thing, right? Like people were congratulating me, and I remember one coworker kept joking like I was some kind of big artist now, which was weird because it was just a display, not like a gallery piece or anything.\n\nBut yeah, since around then, maybe two months, things I used to like just started feeling kind of dull.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still show up to some things, like gallery nights or basketball, but it’s not the same. I used to actually look forward to that stuff.\n\nLike after basketball we’d grab tacos from the food trucks, and there was this one place everyone liked—well, I liked it more than everybody else probably because of the salsa—and I’d be excited about it before. Or helping kids with art projects, that used to feel good.\n\nNow I’m just kind of there… standing around feeling flat, watching everyone else have fun. Then I usually make up some reason to leave early. So I guess I still do things sometimes, but I don’t really feel much pleasure from them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really. I know I already said that, but it’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic.\n\nI still go to stuff sometimes—gallery nights, basketball, those food-truck tacos after. There was this salsa I used to be kind of obsessed with, like I’d make everyone walk farther just to get it. And helping kids with art projects, that used to feel good too.\n\nBut lately I’m just… there. Like I’m standing around flat while everybody else is having a normal time. I usually end up making some excuse to leave early. So I guess I do the activities, but I don’t really feel the enjoyment part anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your current feelings of sadness as overwhelming or interfering significantly with your ability to function?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still functioning, I guess. I still go to work and all that, so it’s not like I’m just completely gone.\n\nBut I’ve been messing things up more. Inventory counts, display plans… stuff I normally could do without thinking too hard. My manager’s been double-checking me, which is embarrassing. He tries to act casual about it, like, “Let’s just look this over,” but I know what that means.\n\nAnd outside work, I’ve been skipping a lot. Gallery nights, basketball, the youth art workshops… I used to be pretty consistent with those. I even had this one kid at the workshop who always wanted to use way too much glitter, like the whole table would be covered in it, and I’d complain but it was kind of funny. Now I just don’t show up half the time.\n\nSo… yeah, I guess it is interfering. Not totally destroying everything, but enough that people notice.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
